   906	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
   907	\section{Evaluation}
   908	\label{sec:eval}
   909	
   910	\subsection{Methodology}
   911	\label{subsec:methodology}
   912	
   913	\paragraph{Suite.} We evaluate on 310 kernel cases across 15 operator
   914	categories (matmul, conv2d, normalization, pooling, elementwise, embedding,
   915	transpose, reshape, etc.). The categories span the operators that dominate
   916	GPU and machine-learning workloads and that kernel generators and autotuners
   917	routinely emit~\cite{tillet2019triton,ouyang2025kernelbench}, so the
   918	obligation load is representative of the code the auditor must serve. 159
   919	cases carry dynamic (symbolic) dimensions, matching real deployments in which
   920	batch and sequence extents are unknown at compile time --- the regime where
   921	boundary masks and shape contracts are most at risk (\S\ref{sec:painpoint}).
   922	All 310 compile; the 21 \texttt{reshape} cases are pure DMA reshapes that
   923	generate no obligations.
   924	
   925	\paragraph{Reproducibility note.} All assessment numbers are collected with a
   926	single pinned compiler binary through the artifact's statistics harness, which
   927	invokes the compiler with absolute input paths. We observed that a handful of
   928	dynamic \texttt{batch\_norm} cases exhibit order-dependent discharge ($\pm 1$
   929	obligation) sensitive to the input path string; the harness configuration
   930	yields stable, reproducible counts. All measurements run on a consumer
   931	workstation (4-core CPU, RTX-class GPU); the artifact contains the full
   932	reproduction pipeline.
   933	
   934	\subsection{RQ1: Discharge Coverage}
   935	\label{subsec:rq1}
   936	
   937	Across the 310 cases, \sys generates 17{,}717 obligations and
   938	discharges 16{,}518 statically (93.2\%). The split by shape kind is the
   939	headline: static-shape kernels discharge 99.2\% (8{,}341/8{,}410 over 151
   940	cases), and dynamic-shape kernels --- where dimensions are symbolic at compile
   941	time --- still discharge 87.9\% (8{,}177/9{,}307 over 159 cases). The residual
   942	1{,}199 obligations are materialized as runtime checks under the cost budget.
   943	Table~\ref{tab:rq1-category} breaks the result down by operator category:
   944	discharge is uniform (87--98\%), with the lowest rates on pooling and softmax,
   945	whose runtime residue is dominated by element-access bounds over symbolic
   946	dimensions.
   947	
   948	\input{tables/rq1_category}
   949	
   950	\subsection{RQ2: Bug Detection Before Execution}
   951	\label{subsec:rq2}
   952	
   953	Refuted obligations surface as compile-time errors and budgeted ones as
   954	launch-time checks, so the auditor doubles as a bug detector at both points.
   955	On the injected-bug suite --- 210 injected defects across dimension-mismatch,
   956	input-dependent out-of-bounds, wrong-output-shape, and stride/layout classes,
   957	the classes that dominate empirical CUDA and tile-program bug
   958	studies~\cite{wu2019cuda,rathnasuriya2026tilebugs} --- \sys catches
   959	\textbf{210/210 before any device execution}, every one refuted statically at
   960	compile time. The same defects lowered to MLIR and IREE pipelines are
   961	detected at 139/210 (80 compile-time, 59 runtime-only) and 80/210 (runtime
   962	entry only) respectively. The comparison is not about engineering quality:
   963	MLIR and IREE are general-purpose pipelines that never see kernel-construct
   964	semantics, and that is precisely the point --- the gap is the assessment
   965	question of \S\ref{subsec:rq6} read from the other side: the defects that
   966	survive lowering-based pipelines are precisely those requiring semantic-stage
   967	structure. Table~\ref{tab:rq2-bugs} breaks detection down by bug class and
   968	stage.
   969	
   970	\input{tables/rq2_bugs}
   971	
   972	\subsection{RQ3: Runtime Cost of Residual Checks}
   973	\label{subsec:rq3}
   974	
   975	We measure end-to-end kernel time for dynamic-shape cases compiled with the
   976	runtime-check threshold at \emph{none} (all residual checks suppressed),
   977	\emph{low}, \emph{medium}, and \emph{high} (all checks), plus the
   978	fully static-shape variant. On the measured subset (70 cases across 7
   979	operator categories, median of 3 runs per variant),
   980	the residual-check overhead is indistinguishable from zero at every level:
   981	$-$0.1\% median at \emph{low} and $-$0.3\% at \emph{high} relative to the
   982	no-checks variant, with per-category medians within $\pm 1.1\%$ and the fully
   983	static-shape variant likewise within noise. Per-case measurements scatter in
   984	both directions ($-$11\% to $+11\%$ at \emph{low}), so we read the residue
   985	cost as at or below the noise floor of these sub-second kernels.
   986	The dominant residual checks are entry-level (64\% of the residue), evaluated
   987	once per launch on the host, so their cost does not scale with problem size.
   988	Table~\ref{tab:rq3-runtime} gives the per-category breakdown.
   989	
   990	\input{tables/rq3_runtime}
   991	
   992	\subsection{RQ4: Compile-Time Overhead}
   993	\label{subsec:rq4}
   994	
   995	Assessment rides on shape inference, so its compile-time cost is small:
   996	across the 151 measured dynamic-shape cases, the median compile-time overhead versus the
   997	fully-static pipeline is \textbf{0.6\%} (mean 1.9\%). The auditor is not an
   998	extra analysis phase; it is the compiler keeping promises it already computes.
   999	
  1000	\subsection{RQ5: Minimality Ablation}
  1001	\label{subsec:rq5}
  1002	
  1003	We remove the value-numbering layer in two independent axes:
  1004	\texttt{--disable-vn-share} gives every expression occurrence a fresh value
  1005	number (no cross-site sharing), and \texttt{--disable-vn-simplify} disables
  1006	the value-number-based simplification rules while keeping constant folding and
  1007	structural normalization. Across all 310 cases the discharge count is
  1008	\emph{identical} in both ablations (16{,}518 discharged in every
  1009	configuration; zero per-case differences): value numbering contributes nothing
  1010	to the outcome. The capability comes from the discipline and the stage, not
  1011	from the analysis machinery. Table~\ref{tab:rq5-ablation} summarizes.
  1012	
  1013	\input{tables/rq5_ablation}
  1014	
  1015	\subsection{RQ6: What Carries the Discharge (Capability Gap)}
  1016	\label{subsec:rq6}
  1017	
  1018	We audited every statically discharged obligation along two axes: the
  1019	\emph{information class} its deciding predicate referenced, and the
  1020	\emph{mechanism} that evaluated it (Table~\ref{tab:rq6-mechanism},
  1021	Figure~\ref{fig:mechanism}). The two axes are not independent measurements,
  1022	and we do not present them as one: a predicate with no symbols is evaluated as
  1023	a constant fold by construction, so the constant-only class and the
  1024	normalization mechanism are two labels on the same population, as are the
  1025	structural class and the interval mechanism. What the audit establishes is
  1026	narrower and more useful --- the shape of the partition itself.
  1027	
  1028	\input{tables/rq6_mechanism}
  1029	
  1030	\begin{figure}[t]
  1031	\centering
  1032	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_mechanism_per_category.pdf}
  1033	\Description[Stacked bar chart of discharge composition per operator
  1034	category]{A horizontal 100-percent stacked bar chart with one bar per
  1035	operator category (fourteen categories, from batch\_norm to transpose). Each
  1036	bar is partitioned into four colour-coded segments whose widths are the share
  1037	of that category's obligations: constant folding over inferred extents (blue),
  1038	interval reasoning over bounded types (yellow), direct static checks (green),
  1039	and the runtime residue (vermilion). The interval segment is present only in
  1040	categories with symbolic parallel structure and is absent in five of the
  1041	fourteen; the runtime residue is a thin segment in most categories and widest
  1042	in max\_pool2d, conv2d, and softmax.}
  1043	\caption{Discharge composition per operator category: constant folds over
  1044	shape-inferred extents, interval reasoning over bounded types (structural),
  1045	direct static checks, and the runtime residue.}
  1046	\label{fig:mechanism}
  1047	\end{figure}
  1048	
  1049	The informative cell is the empty one: \emph{no obligation was discharged by
  1050	pure scalar-symbolic reasoning} (0 of 16{,}518). Whenever a decided predicate
  1051	mentioned a symbol at all, the symbol denoted block/thread-parallel structure,
  1052	and the proof used the bounded types of parallel iterators. Those bounds are
  1053	type-level facts that exist only at the semantic stage; lowering dissolves
  1054	\texttt{p\#n} into flat index arithmetic (\texttt{blockIdx.x} $\cdot\ T + n$)
  1055	from which production compiler pipelines do not
  1056	reconstruct them (\S\ref{subsec:rq2}), and post-hoc reconstruction requires
  1057	SAT-based analysis~\cite{ghosh2026scuba}. The 17.2\%
  1058	structure-dependent discharges --- and, empirically, \emph{all} symbolic
  1059	discharge --- therefore constitute a capability that is available before
  1060	lowering and unavailable after it.
  1061	
  1062	The interval mechanism is also load-bearing within this engine, though the
  1063	argument is structural rather than a second measurement: a predicate that
  1064	still references parallel symbols is not a constant fold, so the only route by
  1065	which it can be discharged is the interval prover. Those 2{,}837 obligations
  1066	were decided by it and by nothing else. Removing it would push all of them into
  1067	the runtime residue --- suite discharge would drop from 93.2\% to 77.2\%
  1068	(dynamic-shape cases: 87.9\% to 74.4\%), and the residue would grow
  1069	3.4$\times$.
  1070	
  1071	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
  1072	\section{Related Work}
  1073	\label{sec:related}
  1074	
  1075	\paragraph{Kernel languages and AI-native compilers.}
  1076	The systems closest to ours keep symbolic shape information but provide no
  1077	audited accounting of which safety obligations are discharged where. Triton
  1078	offers no automatic shape verification; safety assertions are the
  1079	programmer's responsibility. TileLang~\cite{wang2026tilelang} targets
  1080	programmability and performance for neural kernels and leaves checking to the
  1081	underlying pipeline. Halide~\cite{ragan2013halide} infers bounds to serve
  1082	scheduling and buffer sizing, not to audit safety. The \sys surface language
  1083	and its autonomous-tuning pipeline use compile-time \emph{rejection}
  1084	as binary feasibility feedback for a tuning loop, while the present paper
  1085	contributes the per-obligation safety accounting --- generation, discharge,
  1086	refutation, and budgeting --- that the tuning loop implicitly relies on. Code
  1087	generators for tensor cores must still satisfy one narrow hardware contract ---
  1088	the instruction's alignment and shape-divisibility constraints, whose
  1089	violation (e.g., WGMMA shape divisibility) is an empirically documented
  1090	device-specific bug source~\cite{rathnasuriya2026tilebugs}; our hw-constraint
  1091	class generalizes this single contract into a complete, audited obligation
  1092	category.
  1093	
  1094	\paragraph{Graph-level frameworks and IRs.}
  1095	Machine-learning frameworks check shapes at the graph level: TensorFlow
  1096	propagates partial shapes at graph construction, PyTorch traces with fully
  1097	dynamic shapes, and JAX inherits XLA's symbolic shape machinery with
  1098	trace-time checks~\cite{abadi2016tensorflow,paszke2019pytorch,bradbury2018jax,xla}.
  1099	These are per-operator API checks on the graph being built; they say nothing
  1100	about the interior of any kernel. Relay and Relax track symbolic shape
  1101	variables across operators~\cite{roesch2018relay,lai2023relax}, and TVM uses
  1102	them for optimization~\cite{chen2018tvm}, but none maintains an audited
  1103	account of which safety checks are discharged and where. Dynamic-shape compilers such as DISC collect and propagate shape constraints for host--device co-optimization~\cite{zhu2021disc}, again at graph granularity. MLIR carries the most developed IR-level shape machinery:
  1104	its affine dialect supports Presburger reasoning over loop bounds and
  1105	indices~\cite{lattner2021mlir}. It operates after semantic lowering, however,
  1106	by which point cross-tensor contracts (``the contraction dims of a matmul
  1107	must agree'') have dissolved into scalar index arithmetic; IREE adds
  1108	host-side entry assertions but no interior checks~\cite{iree}.
  1109	
  1110	\paragraph{Bounds-check elimination and assertion systems.}
  1111	Static, dynamic, and hybrid elimination of array bounds checks is classical.
  1112	ABCD eliminates programmer-written checks on demand via inequality
  1113	propagation over SSA graphs~\cite{bodik2000abcd}; dependent-type systems
  1114	eliminate them statically through proof-carrying
  1115	annotations~\cite{xi1998eliminating,xi1999dependent}. We differ in both
  1116	directions from this line of work: obligations are \emph{generated} from
  1117	kernel-construct semantics rather than written by programmers, and discharge
  1118	happens while parallel structure is still visible as type-level facts rather
  1119	than over a general-purpose IR. SCuBA demonstrates the cost of working after
  1120	the fact: to find elusive out-of-bounds accesses in CUDA programs it must
  1121	\emph{reconstruct} semantic relations among allocation sizes, grid
  1122	dimensions, and offsets from program code, discharging them with a SAT
  1123	solver~\cite{ghosh2026scuba}. Our RQ6 audit (\S\ref{subsec:rq6}) shows why
  1124	this reconstruction is expensive: the relations SCuBA solves for exist as
  1125	declared iterator bounds before lowering, where two classical mechanisms
  1126	settle them without a solver.
  1127	
  1128	\paragraph{Dependent types and shape-typed array languages.}
  1129	Staged shape-dependent
  1130	types~\cite{suwa2026staged} check tensor shapes at compile time through
  1131	staging; size-dependent type systems for GPU array
  1132	languages follow the same discipline~\cite{henriksen2021size,bailly2023shape},
  1133	building on the
  1134	classical dependent-typing line~\cite{xi1998eliminating,xi1999dependent}.
  1135	Refinement types~\cite{vazou2014refinement} generalize further still. The shared cost is a
  1136	programmer-facing type discipline: shape constraints must be expressible in
  1137	the type language, inference is best-effort, and failure is a type error ---
  1138	there is no notion of a budgeted, accounted runtime residue. Our obligations
  1139	require no type-level programming: they are generated from constructs and
  1140	discharged as a byproduct of inference the compiler already performs.
  1141	
  1142	\paragraph{Contracts and gradual checking.}
  1143	Software contracts~\cite{findler2002contracts} and gradual typing with
  1144	blame~\cite{siek2006gradual,wadler2009blame} explore the same static/dynamic
  1145	boundary as our cost-gated materialization, at the granularity of annotated
  1146	program points; gradual tensor shape checking~\cite{hattori2023gradual} and
  1147	gradual shape analysis for ML frameworks~\cite{migeed2024shape} apply this to
  1148	tensor shapes specifically. Gradual verification~\cite{bader2018gradual}
  1149	applies the same static/dynamic split to correctness specifications, letting a
  1150	program carry both statically-verified and dynamically-checked parts. The
  1151	auditor differs in what crosses the boundary and how it is tracked: the
  1152	residue is split \emph{per generated obligation} (not per annotated program
  1153	point), priced by a cost model, and the split is machine-checkable in the
  1154	ledger.
  1155	
  1156	\paragraph{Verification and testing of GPU kernels.}
  1157	The classical line checks CUDA source by concolic test
  1158	generation~\cite{li2012gklee} or by SMT-based deductive verification over
  1159	annotated programs~\cite{betts2012gpuverify};
  1160	ProofWright makes this agentic, inferring verifier annotations for
  1161	LLM-generated kernels at about three minutes per
  1162	kernel~\cite{chatterjee2025proofwright}; and contract-style adversarial
  1163	testing hardens the runtime oracle itself, finding that 39.5\% of
  1164	harness-accepted LLM kernels are
  1165	broken~\cite{shah2026contract}. Controlled studies corroborate that the
  1166	fixed-shape allclose oracle certifies silently-wrong
  1167	kernels~\cite{sarkar2026correctness}. All of this work is
  1168	post-hoc and external to the compiler: obligations are written or inferred
  1169	per kernel, at per-kernel cost, with no total accounting of what was checked.
  1170	The auditor's obligations are generated by the compiler that already knows
  1171	the constructs, and most are discharged for free during compilation.
  1172	Equivalence checkers for tensor-program
  1173	optimizers---superoptimizer verifiers~\cite{wu2025mirage} and graph-rewrite
  1174	verifiers such as TensorRight~\cite{arora2025tensorright}---answer a different
  1175	question, that a transformation preserves semantics, at search time rather
  1176	than compile time, and say nothing about the safety of the kernel itself.
  1177	
  1178	\paragraph{Empirical studies of compiler bugs.}
  1179	Recent studies characterize the failures the auditor targets ---
  1180	tile-program codegen bugs coupled to input shapes, types, and backend
  1181	targets~\cite{rathnasuriya2026tilebugs}, and analogous bugs in FPGA
  1182	high-level-synthesis tools~\cite{wang2025bambu} --- and call for
  1183	in-compiler checking; the auditor is that tooling.
  1184	
  1185	\paragraph{Compiler trust and certified artifacts.}
  1186	Orthogonal to verifying kernels is verifying the compiler itself. The
  1187	certified-compiler line proves correctness of compilation end-to-end, as in
  1188	CompCert~\cite{leroy2009formal}, while proof-carrying
  1189	code~\cite{necula1997pcc} ships a machine-checkable proof that generated code
  1190	satisfies a safety policy. The auditor occupies a cheaper point between these
  1191	two: it neither proves the compiler nor carries full program proofs, but
  1192	produces a ledger accounting for every generated obligation, so a consumer can
  1193	audit \emph{what was checked where} without trusting the discharge machinery.
  1194	
  1195	\paragraph{Runtime GPU safety.}
  1196	Empirical studies of CUDA programs document memory- and shape-related
  1197	defects as a recurring bug class~\cite{wu2019cuda}. Runtime tools such as
  1198	compute-sanitizer~\cite{nvidia2024sanitizer} and hardware-assisted schemes
  1199	such as GPUArmor~\cite{ziad2025gpuarmor} catch violations dynamically, only on
  1200	exercised inputs and at execution cost; Tensfa detects and repairs
  1201	tensor shape faults at the framework level~\cite{wu2021tensfa}. These tools
  1202	are complements, not competitors: proven obligations need no runtime checks
  1203	at all, and the budgeted residue pinpoints exactly where dynamic checking
  1204	remains necessary.
  1205

  1244	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
  1245	\section{Conclusion}
  1246	\label{sec:conclusion}
  1247	
  1248	We have presented the compiler-as-auditor contract for GPU kernels: safety
  1249	obligations generated completely from construct and target semantics,
  1250	discharged by deliberately classical machinery --- constant folding over
  1251	shape-inferred extents and interval reasoning over bounded types --- and
  1252	accounted for in a machine-checkable ledger where every obligation is proven,
  1253	refuted, or cost-budgeted. The evidence over 310 kernels: 93.2\% static discharge, 100\%
  1254	detection of injected shape bugs before device execution, residual checks
  1255	at noise-floor
  1256	cost, and an information-dependence audit showing that every symbolic
  1257	discharge relies on parallel structure that lowering destroys.
  1258	
  1259	Limitations. The evaluation targets one kernel compiler on NVIDIA GPUs, and
  1260	the discharge engine is intentionally incomplete: predicates outside
  1261	normalization and interval arithmetic fall to the runtime residue, whose
  1262	coverage is traded against an explicit cost budget. Runtime overhead is
  1263	measured on sub-second kernels whose residue is dominated by host-side entry
  1264	checks; kernels with device-heavy residues or much shorter runtimes may see
  1265	relatively higher check costs. The audit is limited to
  1266	the obligation classes of \S\ref{sec:ledger} --- concurrency safety and
  1267	numeric correctness are out of scope --- and assessment arithmetic assumes
  1268	mathematical integers. The broader claim for the ecosystem stands on the
  1269	discipline, not the machinery: kernel DSLs already impose exactly the
  1270	structure this discipline needs --- any of them can be an auditor.

     1	# Experiment Re-Design Plan — Proving the Safety Ledger
     2	
     3	**Date:** 2026-09-08 · **Window:** 48 h · **Mode:** multi-agent parallel
     4	**Owner review state:** DRAFT v2 — restructured per owner feedback; awaiting sign-off.
     5	
     6	> Purpose. The paper's thesis is *the compiler as a safety ledger*. Today the
     7	> evaluation proves **discharge strength** (RQ1) and **cost** (RQ3/RQ4), but only
     8	*partially* proves **detection** (RQ2 mutates 3 of 4 obligation classes) and
     9	> *not at all* proves **generation completeness**. This plan closes both gaps and
    10	> makes every axis comparable — where a comparison is meaningful — so each ledger
    11	> claim is backed by a measured result rather than asserted.
    12	
    13	---
    14	
    15	## 0. The thesis, restated as four falsifiable claims
    16	
    17	A "safety ledger" is a claim of **complete, countable accounting**. That claim
    18	decomposes into exactly four testable propositions.
    19	
    20	| # | Claim (what "ledger" means) | Experiment | SOTA comparison? | Status today |
    21	|---|---|---|---|---|
    22	| **C1** | It *catches more bugs* when we mutate (detection completeness) | E1 mutation | Triton, MLIR(linalg+low), IREE, TileLang* | **partial** — 3 classes mutated (M1/M2/M3), no SOTA side-by-side |
    23	| **C2** | It *generates the full safety assessment* per operator (generation completeness) | E2 enumeration | qualitative only (see §4) | **absent** |
    24	| **C3** | It *resolves more* of them statically (discharge strength) | E3 remainder | MLIR(linalg+low), IREE | partial — RQ1 exists, no side-by-side |
    25	| **C4** | It does so at *minor cost* (cheap → deployable) | E4 + E5 | **choreo only** | **partial** — E4 (RQ4) done; E5 new |
    26	
    27	The through-line: **one operator, one ledger** — for the same kernel, our
    28	compiler enumerates every obligation, settles most, and accounts for the rest.
    29	Two axes (C1, C3) are SOTA-comparable; C2 is *choreo-exact but SOTA-qualitative*
    30	(because other systems evaluate checks in place and keep no count); C4 is a
    31	choreo-only property (the ledger's own overhead, not a cross-tool comparison).
    32	
    33	---
    34	
    35	## 1. SOTA baseline matrix
    36	
    37	One operator must be lowered through **each** pipeline so the axes are measured
    38	against the *same program*, not different programs. Per owner guidance, **MLIR is
    39	split into two comparison targets** with different jobs (see §3.3):
    40	
    41	| Pipeline | What it can express | What it checks | Role |
    42	|---|---|---|---|
    43	| **Triton** (de-facto DSL) | block-level tensors; bounds only as manual `mask` values | nothing (mask = user value, not a generated check) | C1 dynamic-OOB; C2 qualitative floor |
    44	| **MLIR — linalg level** | high-level *shape* consistency (tensor/linalg named ops, shape inference) | rank/shape agreement between tensors; no memory or loop concern | C1 **shape-compatibility** mutations; C3 high-level discharge |
    45	| **MLIR — low level** (`scf`/`affine`/`memref`/`vector`/`gpu`) | affine loop bounds, memory access indices, vector tile constraints | affine bounds, `vector.transfer_read` masks, `memref` index legality | C1 **element-access**; C3 low-level discharge |
    46	| **IREE** (end-to-end) | `stream`/`hal` runtime | host-side **entry shape checks only**, no interior checks | C1/C3 "production entry checks" |
    47	| **TileLang** (shape-aware DSL, *exploratory*) | tile-level programs with explicit shape/loop structure | partial static shape reasoning | C1/C2 **exploratory** — run it; disclosure decided at integration (§11.7) |
    48	| **compute-sanitizer** (runtime, NVIDIA) | instruments the *generated* CUDA/PTX/SASS, not the source | dynamic, exercised-input only | C1 **dynamic-oracle** baseline (§3.5) + C4 *separate* cost line (§6) |
    49	
    50	**Compute-sanitizer: no separate pure-CUDA codebase.** compute-sanitizer
    51	instruments the *compiled device code* (PTX/SASS at load time), so it runs on
    52	whatever the frontend emitted — Triton's JIT output, IREE's generated CUDA, or
    53	choreo's own lowered CUDA — with no hand-written CUDA kernel needed. Feasibility
    54	is high (run the Triton script under `compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck`); the
    55	limit is coverage, not setup: it sees only *exercised* accesses, so it is silent
    56	on shape contracts, never-taken loops, and empty iteration spaces. That is why it
    57	is a C1 **dynamic-oracle baseline**, never a C2/C3 comparison — and why running
    58	it over the SOTA targets (not choreo) yields the "what a developer's runtime
    59	checker adds on top of bare SOTA" delta measured in §3.5.
    60	
    61	**TileLang: exploratory, hold-back lane.** Run the full E1/E2 on TileLang. Its
    62	results are *not auto-included*: at integration (§8 Phase 2) the owner decides
    63	whether to expose the column. Its per-class mutation mapping (its surface differs
    64	from Triton/MLIR) is pinned by the `coordinator` in Phase 0, not guessed here.
    65	
    66	**Key fairness principle (unchanged from RQ2):** the comparison measures *where
    67	safety information exists*, not engineering quality. The linalg/low-level split
    68	is itself part of the thesis: shape obligations live at one level, memory
    69	obligations at another, and no single MLIR level sees the whole set — whereas
    70	choreo's ledger sees all four classes in one place.
    71	
    72	---
    73	
    74	## 2. Shared benchmark: ALL 15 categories — scope-split by axis
    75	
    76	### 2.1 The suite (complete for E2/E3; a coverage subset for E1)
    77	The **full 15-category suite** is the benchmark for the *breadth* axes — E2
    78	generation and E3 discharge — where the "93.2% of 17,717 obligations across 15
    79	categories" claim lives, and where `--dump-ledger` makes breadth nearly free.
    80	E1 (mutation) does **not** span all 15; it runs over a **minimal coverage subset**
    81	(§3.1) because detection is keyed to obligation class, not operator. The suite:
    82	
    83	`batch_norm`, `concat`, `conv2d`, `elemwise_add`, `embedding`, `gelu`,
    84	`layer_normalization`, `matmul`, `max_pool2d`, `reduce_mean`, `relu`, `reshape`,
    85	`sigmoid`, `softmax`, `transpose`.
    86	
    87	*(The owner's category list also mentioned "scripts" — treated here as the
    88	harness/composition directory, **not** an operator. Flagged for confirmation in
    89	Appendix B.)*
    90	
    91	### 2.2 Source is NOT shared — agents *compose* kernels
    92	Only the **operator settings** (input shapes, tensor contracts, reference
    93	semantics) are shared. The benchmark *source* is not handed over. Each agent
    94	**composes its own kernels** from the settings, which (a) keeps the comparison
    95	independent and (b) exercises each toolchain's real authoring path. The choreo
    96	kernel for a category is the *reference semantics*, not a copy source — Triton/
    97	MLIR/IREE versions are independently written *lowered equivalents* carrying the
    98	same operator semantics.
    99	
   100	### 2.3 Per-category lifecycle (development → compile → run)
   101	Every category, in every toolchain, must pass three gates before its numbers are
   102	recorded:
   103	
   104	1. **Development** — compose the kernel from the shared settings.
   105	2. **Compile test** — the kernel must lower/compile cleanly (this is where
   106	   compile-time detection is recorded).
   107	3. **Run test** — the kernel must execute on device and produce a
   108	   reference-checked output (this is where runtime-caught and never-detected
   109	   outcomes are recorded, and where silent corruption is confirmed — see §11.1).
   110	
   111	### 2.4 Small-input guard (save execution time)
   112	Large-input kernels (e.g. `conv2d`, `matmul`, `batch_norm` over big tensors) must
   113	ship a **`--small` / minor-input mode** that substitutes a tiny-but-legal input
   114	shape for *exploration* (development + compile + a fast correctness smoke run).
   115	The **full-size** run is reserved for the *final recorded number* only. Conventions
   116	(what "small" means per operator, and that small/full must agree semantically)
   117	are pinned by the `coordinator` in Phase 0.
   118	
   119	### 2.5 The `benchmark2/` directory (derived from the original benchmark)
   120	All new work lives in a **`benchmark2/`** tree, *derived from* the original
   121	`benchmark/` so the original is never mutated in place. Derivation rule: extract
   122	**settings only** (shapes, contracts, reference semantics) — never the source —
   123	and compose kernels fresh in each toolchain subdir. Provenance is recorded so
   124	every `benchmark2` artifact traces back to its original case.
   125	
   126	```
   127	benchmark2/
   128	  settings/            # shared operator settings (the ONLY thing handed to workers)
   129	  specs/               # M1/M2/M3 mutation specs + §3.3 translation table (coordinator pins)
   130	  choreo/              # choreo kernels + --dump-ledger dumps
   131	  triton/  mlir-linalg/  mlir-low/  iree/  tilelang/   # per-toolchain kernels
   132	  results/             # per-toolchain JSON: 3-way outcomes + gen-capability + remainder
   133	  manifest.md          # versions/flags + derivation/provenance map to original benchmark
   134	```
   135	- `settings/` is the single source of truth for shapes/contracts; workers read
   136	  only this and compose independently (§2.2).
   137	- Each `results/<toolchain>/` subdir emits the JSON defined in §11.3; tables in
   138	  `main.tex` are *generated* from these, never hand-edited.
   139	- `manifest.md` records, per setting, the original `benchmark/` case it was
   140	  derived from (path + hash), so a reviewer can re-derive `benchmark2` and confirm
   141	  no source leaked across toolchains.
   142	
   143	---
   144	
   145	## 3. E1 — Mutation completeness (fills the C1 gap)
   146	
   147	### 3.1 Goal and scope (depth over breadth)
   148	Reproduce the paper's injected-bug suite — the **4 bug categories it actually
   149	reports** (`dimension-mismatch`, `input-dependent OOB`, `wrong-output-shape`,
   150	`stride/layout error`), grouped into their **3 obligation classes** (plus the
   151	paper's own `hw-constraint` class, which §3.2 justifies as an in-scope mutation
   152	even though RQ2 injected no bug in it) — with **equal mutation counts per
   153	class**, and record a **3-way outcome per mutant**:
   154	**compile-time detected / runtime caught / never detected**. A fourth cell
   155	**n/a (surface cannot express)** is allowed and recorded explicitly — never
   156	merged into "detected."
   157	
   158	**choreo detects at *both* stages — by design, not a fallback.** A static-shape
   159	obligation is refuted at compile time; a dynamic-shape obligation whose bounds
   160	depend on runtime values is *materialized* as a runtime entry check and caught at
   161	launch. choreo's `c`/`r` split therefore follows the **static-vs-dynamic shape**
   162	boundary of the obligation, not its class: M1 OOB is `c` when index bounds are
   163	statically known and `r` when they are data-dependent, and the same holds for
   164	M2. The runtime checks are *mostly* (not exclusively) a dynamic-shape
   165	mechanism. Every choreo cell must be reported as a `c + r = N, never = 0` split,
   166	never collapsed to a single stage.
   167	
   168	**Mutations do NOT run across all 15 categories.** Detection (C1) is a property
   169	of the *obligation class*, not the operator: the element-access checker is the
   170	same machinery whether it checks `relu`, `gelu`, `sigmoid`, or `elemwise_add`, so
   171	mutating four unary elementwise ops is four copies of one piece of evidence.
   172	Statistical power comes from *mutant diversity within a class*, not from
   173	repeating the class across operators. Expressibility is also non-uniform —
   174	`reshape` (0 obligations) has nothing to mutate for M1/M2/M3 — so an "all 15 ×
   175	both classes" matrix would be mostly n/a cells at ~3–6× the cost.
   176	
   177	E1 therefore runs over a **minimal coverage set** — the smallest operator subset
   178	in which every obligation class is exercised by at least one operator — each
   179	chosen for the distinct defect it exposes:
   180	
   181	| Obligation class | Paper bug category | Mutate on | Why (the distinct defect it exposes) |
   182	|---|---|---|---|
   183	| M1 element-access | `input-dependent OOB`, `stride/layout error` | `layer_norm`, `softmax`, `relu`, `transpose` | dynamic-mask OOB, dynamic-reduce OOB, static-elemwise OOB, **strided/transposed layout OOB** |
   184	| M2 shape-compat | `dimension-mismatch`, `wrong-output-shape` | `layer_norm`, `matmul`, `concat` | scale-contract, matmul K/N contract, multi-input shape, **wrong/partial output write** |
   185	| M3 hw-constraint | *(paper §ledger: alignment, atom divisibility; no RQ2 bug)* | `matmul`, `conv2d` | **MMA contraction-dim divisibility, tensor-core alignment** |
   186	
   187	(≈ 7 operators, 2–3 per class; `layer_norm` anchors M1+M2 and is the running
   188	example; `matmul` anchors M2+M3.) The full 15-category suite is still composed
   189	once per toolchain for E2/E3; only the *injection* step is scoped to this set.
   190	
   191	*Iteration-validity (the paper's 3rd class, `foreach`/`parallel` → $L>0$,
   192	stride$\neq$0) is **not** given its own lane: its two observable failures —
   193	zero/empty iteration space and zero stride — are injected through M1's
   194	`stride/layout` spec and M3's divisibility spec, and a loop-level `parallel by
   195	0` has no portable Triton/MLIR analogue (§3.3).*
   196	
   197	**Level-2 candidates (add only if time is feasible, after the minimal set is
   198	green).** Each entry adds *defect diversity* — a new failure mode — rather than a
   199	repeat of the same checker, so its marginal evidence is real but secondary. Pull
   200	in **priority order**, highest first:
   201	
   202	| Priority | Class | Add operator(s) | Additional evidence it contributes |
   203	|---|---|---|---|
   204	| 1 | M1 | `max_pool2d`, `conv2d` | window OOB; strided multi-dim index OOB |
   205	| 2 | M2 | `elemwise_add`, `softmax` | broadcast/rank mismatch; axis contract |
   206	| 3 | M1 | `embedding`, `batch_norm` | lookup OOB; channel-index OOB |
   207	| 4 | M3 | `batch_norm` | channel-alignment / vectorization-width constraint |
   208	
   209	Level-2 is strictly additive: it never changes the minimal-set results, only
   210	widens the "which operators" column. Pull order follows the §8.1 **work cycle**
   211	— *level-1 E1 → other work (E2/E3 breadth) → level-2 E1* — and a level-2
   212	operator is pulled only when the minimal set for its class is fully green (all
   213	cells classified, JSON validated) **and** the breadth work is done. A worker
   214	with idle time takes the next priority row, never an arbitrary operator.
   215	
   216	### 3.2 The four mutation *specs* (shared), and their choreo syntax (reference)
   217	
   218	Mutations are specified **conceptually** (a defect spec) so the *same defect* is
   219	re-expressed in each toolchain's own idiom. Equal counts per class are fixed at
   220	spec level; a mutant that a surface cannot express is marked n/a there, not
   221	re-balanced.
   222	
   223	**M1 — element-access.** *paper: `input-dependent OOB` (58) + `stride/layout error` (5).*
   224	- dropped boundary mask; `p#n` off-by-one; negative index;
   225	  **transposed/non-contiguous stride; offset view; zero-stride / empty range**
   226	  (the observable residue of iteration-validity, folded here — see §3.1).
   227	
   228	**M2 — shape-compatibility.** *paper: `dimension-mismatch` (139) + `wrong-output-shape` (8).*
   229	- caller passes `scale` of `L-1`; DMA src/dst extent disagreement; binary op on
   230	  mismatched shapes; **output tensor declared with a wrong leading extent;
   231	  partial/duplicate write (omitted tail or overlapping tile)**.
   232	
   233	**M3 — hardware-constraint.** *paper §ledger: `target ops → alignment, atom
   234	divisibility`; no RQ2 bug category, but in scope by the §ledger table and
   235	documented empirically by rathnasuriya2026tilebugs' device-specific class (WGMMA
   236	shape divisibility) and resource-allocation class (alignment).*
   237	- MMA contraction dim not divisible by the tensor-core atom (`K % 16 ≠ 0`);
   238	  misaligned tensor-core/DMA base address; shared-memory tile exceeding the
   239	  device limit.
   240	
   241	*Deliberately out of scope, named so the exclusion is auditable (already stated
   242	in main.tex §ledger): **concurrency safety** (races, missing barriers —
   243	wu2019cuda, GPUArmor, GPUVerify) and **numeric correctness** (mixed-precision
   244	casts, NaN/inf/signed-zero, accumulation drift — rathnasuriya2026tilebugs'
   245	type-and-operator class, islam2019dlbugs' logic bugs). These two families are
   246	the other silent-bug classes the cited related work reports; they are excluded
   247	here by the paper's own scope statement, not overlooked.*
   248	
   249	### 3.3 Per-surface adaptation (mutations are NOT syntax-identical)
   250	The owner's point is explicit here: **`parallel p by 0` does not exist in Triton
   251	or MLIR** — parallelism is expressed differently (`program_id`/`num_programs`,
   252	`scf.parallel`, `gpu.launch`). So each spec is *translated* to the surface's own
   253	mechanism for the same conceptual defect. The translation table the agents must
   254	follow:
   255	
   256	| Defect class | choreo (ref) | Triton | MLIR — linalg | MLIR — low level | IREE |
   257	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
   258	| M1 OOB | `a.at(i)` | omit/`tl.load` mask | n/a (no memory) | `affine`/`memref` index out of range | entry shape only |
   259	| M2 shape | DMA extent mismatch | n/a (no cross-tensor contract) | **rank/shape mismatch** (primary target) | n/a | entry shape check |
   260	| M3 hw | `mma`/`tma` alignment, atom divisibility | partial (`tl.dot` K divisibility) | n/a | partial (`vector`/`affine` alignment) | n/a |
   261	
   262	**The MLIR/linalg split in E1:** linalg is compared only against **M2 (shape)**
   263	— it checks high-level shape consistency and nothing else. The low-level MLIR is
   264	compared against **M1** — it focuses on memory access. The two MLIR targets
   265	therefore get **different, non-overlapping mutation sets**, and their results are
   266	reported separately (never summed into one "MLIR" row).
   267	
   268	### 3.4 Output
   269	Replace `tab:rq2-bugs` with a 3-class (M1/M2 = 4 paper categories, M3 = hw) ×
   270	toolchain table whose cells are the 3-way outcome (compile / runtime / never),
   271	with n/a where the surface can't express the class. Sketch:
   272	
   273	```
   274	Bug class        n  choreo (c+r)    Triton    MLIR-linalg  MLIR-low   IREE
   275	M1 OOB/stride    N  c+r=N, never=0  -/x/N     n/a          x/y/N      -/x/N
   276	M2 shape         N  c+r=N, never=0  n/a       c/x/N        n/a        -/x/N
   277	M3 hw            N  c+r=N, never=0  partial   n/a          partial    n/a
   278	```
   279	(c = compile-detected, r = runtime-caught, N = never, n/a = cannot express.
   280	For choreo the c/r split follows the static-vs-dynamic shape boundary (§3.1);
   281	never = 0 by construction. Cells filled from measurement, not assumption.
   282	Each row aggregates the paper's four bug categories — M1 covers
   283	`input-dependent OOB` + `stride/layout error`, M2 covers `dimension-mismatch` +
   284	`wrong-output-shape` — and the paper's per-category counts (58/5, 139/8) are
   285	reproduced in the `n` column alongside the equal-count re-injection.)
   286	
   287	### 3.5 compute-sanitizer: the developer-practice *dynamic oracle* (supplement)
   288	
   289	A reviewer will ask "your SOTA baselines don't catch these bugs — but a CUDA
   290	developer would just run compute-sanitizer; doesn't that close the gap?" So we
   291	**measure it**, and the answer becomes a paper result instead of a defensive
   292	paragraph.
   293	
   294	**Method.** Run `compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck` over the *same generated
   295	CUDA/PTX* each non-choreo toolchain already emits (Triton JIT output, MLIR→LLVM,
   296	IREE generated CUDA, TileLang). No new codebase — this is a flag on top of work
   297	already being done. It is run **on the SOTA targets, not choreo**: choreo already
   298	carries its own runtime residue checks *and* compile-time discharge, so sanitizer
   299	over choreo is confounded (belt-and-suspenders) and adds nothing to the claim.
   300	
   301	**What it measures — the *supplement*.** For each injected mutant, "does a
   302	developer's runtime memory checker catch what the bare toolchain missed?" i.e.
   303	the delta `bare SOTA` → `SOTA + compute-sanitizer`.
   304	
   305	**What it can and cannot see — this is the result.** compute-sanitizer is a
   306	*dynamic memory-error oracle*: it reports only accesses that (a) are an actual
   307	OOB/uninitialized/misaligned fault **and** (b) execute on the given input. Mapped
   308	onto the mutation classes, it is predictably blind to the ledger's core wins:
   309	
   310	| Class | compute-sanitizer outcome | Why |
   311	|---|---|---|
   312	| M1 exercised OOB | **flags** | a real OOB fault that actually runs |
   313	| M1 never-taken path | **silent (false −)** | the fault never executes on this input |
   314	| M2 shape-contract | **silent** | a DMA-extent disagreement that happens not to deref OOB is a semantic contract, not a memory fault |
   315	
   316	So the supplement column is expected to catch the *exercised M1* slice of the
   317	injected suite and **nothing in M2** — the exact classes the ledger discharges
   318	statically. That gap (`ledger − sanitizer` = never-taken paths, semantic
   319	contracts) *is* the paper's contribution made concrete, and it is a fair,
   320	familiar baseline (no strawman: this is what every developer actually runs).
   321	
   322	**Output.** Add a `compute-sanitizer` column to the §3.4 table (S12 in §12.4),
   323	covering the SOTA toolchains only. The run's own time is irrelevant here — E1
   324	observes *flag / no-flag*, not timing (§6 covers the cost question).
   325	
   326	---
   327	
   328	## 4. E2 — Generation completeness (choreo-exact; SOTA-qualitative)
   329	
   330	### 4.1 The owner's constraint
   331	Other systems evaluate safety assessments **in place** and keep **no exact
   332	generation count** — a Triton mask is a value, an affine bound is folded during
   333	canonicalization, so "how many checks were generated" is not recoverable as a
   334	number. E2 therefore does **not** force a per-toolchain count.
   335	
   336	### 4.2 What E2 proves, and how
   337	- **choreo (quantitative, exact):** `--dump-ledger` enumerates the *complete*
   338	  obligation set per operator (e.g. `layer_norm` = 79 = 72 elem + 5 shape + 2
   339	  loop). This is the **generation-completeness proof** — the ledger is the
   340	  account of every obligation, by construction.
   341	- **SOTA (qualitative, expressibility):** for each toolchain, record *which of
   342	  the four obligation classes it can even express* (from §3.3), as a
   343	  **generation-capability matrix**. No number is claimed; a toolchain that cannot
   344	  express a class is recorded as "**not suitable for generation comparison** on
   345	  this class," not given a fabricated zero.
   346	
   347	The claim the paper carries: *choreo is the only target that generates the full
   348	assessment set as an explicit, enumerable object*; the others generate a partial,
   349	implicit set that cannot be counted. That is a stronger and defensible claim than
   350	"we generate more."
   351	
   352	### 4.3 Output
   353	A compact generation-capability table (`tab:gen-capability`): rows = 4 classes,
   354	columns = choreo/Triton/MLIR-linalg/MLIR-low/IREE, cells = "enumerable count"
   355	(choreo) vs "expressible / inexpressible / partial" (SOTA). `layer_norm`'s 79 is
   356	the worked example lifted into §3 prose.
   357	
   358	---
   359	
   360	## 5. E3 — Discharge strength via *remainders* (gen+resolve compatibility)
   361	
   362	### 5.1 The owner's constraint
   363	Because generation count is unknown in SOTA, E3 cannot compute
   364	"resolved / generated." It measures **remainders** instead — the obligations left
   365	*unresolved/unconditional* — which is observable in every system even when the
   366	generation count is not.
   367	
   368	### 5.2 Remainder = generation − resolution (the observable part)
   369	- **choreo**: remainder is explicit and accounted — 1,199 of 17,717
   370	  (6.8%) are runtime-materialized or budgeted-not-emitted, **0 silently dropped**.
   371	- **MLIR — low level**: when array bounds checks are enabled, the bounds that
   372	  cannot be proven remain as **unconditional runtime guards** — count them as the
   373	  remainder. Many will be unconditional (the owner's point) because the
   374	  structural information that choreo's interval/canonical reasoning uses was
   375	  already lost in lowering.
   376	- **MLIR — linalg**: high-level shape checks that fail to fold remain as dynamic
   377	  shape assertions.
   378	- **IREE**: entry shape checks that are not statically foldable are the remainder.
   379	
   380	**Interpretation (gen+resolve compatibility):** a system is a *good ledger* only
   381	if it both generates the set **and** resolves most of it. choreo's residue is
   382	small *and* accounted; a target that leaves a large unconditional remainder
   383	demonstrates the gap directly — it can emit checks but not prove them, or it
   384	proves them at a level where the structure is already gone.
   385	
   386	### 5.3 Keep the existing counterfactual
   387	The **no-interval → 93.2%→77.2%** (dynamic 87.9%→74.4%, residue ×3.4) result is
   388	the internal "we resolve more because of the semantic stage" evidence, retained
   389	as the mechanism attribution.
   390	
   391	### 5.4 Output
   392	Remainder counts folded into the per-operator table (`tab:per-operator`), sharing
   393	page budget with E2.
   394	
   395	---
   396	
   397	## 6. Cost (choreo only — C4)
   398	
   399	Cost is a **choreo-only measurement**: the ledger's own overhead, not a
   400	cross-toolchain property. It splits into two *non-overlapping* experiments — the
   401	one-time **compile** cost (E4) and the per-run **runtime** cost (E5). The split is
   402	forced by a fact of the design: runtime checks exist **only on dynamic-shape
   403	cases**, because static-shape cases discharge 100% of obligations at compile time
   404	and emit *no* runtime residue. "Runtime cost" is therefore, by construction, a
   405	dynamic-shape-only phenomenon and must not be smeared into a single average that
   406	hides this.
   407	
   408	**Both E4 and E5 are required C4 results.** The runtime experiments are
   409	long-running, so they are scheduled for the `choreo` worker's dedicated GPU time
   410	(§8.1) and are **required to be invoked** within the 48 h. E5 runs after E1–E4
   411	are green; RQ3's runtime figure is then refined by the E5a A/B rather than
   412	re-reported as-is.
   413	
   414	### 6.1 E4 — Compile-time cost
   415	RQ4 (0.6% median compile overhead) stands. This is the *one-time* price of
   416	generating + discharging the ledger, paid once per kernel and independent of how
   417	many inputs later run. It is host-CPU wall-clock, not GPU-contended; record it
   418	under quiet host load.
   419	
   420	### 6.2 E5 — Runtime detection cost & latency (dynamic-shape only)
   421	
   422	> **Required lane.** E5 is choreo work on the C4 critical path (§6 intro,
   423	> §8.1). The `e5` subcommand must exist, be correct, **and be invoked** after
   424	> E1–E4 are green.
   425	
   426	**Why this is measurable at all.** choreo's runtime residue checks are *generated
   427	code*, so they can be compiled out (`--runtime-checks off`). That gives a clean
   428	A/B the other tools cannot offer — the same kernel, with and without its checks,
   429	diffed at runtime — and because checks exist only on dynamic-shape cases, the A/B
   430	is scoped there.
   431	
   432	**The mechanism asymmetry (the owner's point).** choreo hoists its dynamic-shape
   433	obligations to *entry* checks — it validates shape bounds before the kernel body
   434	runs, so a mutated dynamic-shape case fails at launch, before any real work.
   435	compute-sanitizer is a dynamic oracle: it instruments every access and reports
   436	only when the faulting access *executes*, so it must run the instrumented body to
   437	the fault. The two have **categorically different detection latency**.
   438	
   439	**E5a — per-run residue overhead (the honest RQ3).** On each dynamic-shape case,
   440	run twice — `--runtime-checks on` vs `--runtime-checks off` — and take the Δ.
   441	This refines RQ3's −0.1%/−0.3% noise-floor: it is now reported *per dynamic-shape
   442	case* (and is 0 by construction on static-shape cases), not smeared across the
   443	suite.
   444	
   445	**E5b — detection latency vs the dynamic oracle.** On E1's `r`-outcome
   446	(dynamic-shape) mutants, measure time-from-launch-to-report for (i) choreo's entry
   447	check, and (ii) compute-sanitizer over choreo's generated CUDA with
   448	`--runtime-checks off` (so sanitizer is the *sole* detector, not racing choreo's
   449	own check). This is distinct from §3.5's SOTA supplement: here sanitizer runs over
   450	choreo's *own* code to isolate the latency model. Expected: choreo ≈ launch +
   451	entry-check eval (µs-scale, constant); sanitizer ≈ launch + instrumented body up
   452	to fault (data-dependent, grows with fault depth). Record *where* choreo's check
   453	fires (entry vs interior) as a measured field, not an assumption.
   454	
   455	**Fairness (do not strawman).** compute-sanitizer is a *debugger* — it is not
   456	trying to be fast, so "always high cost?" is the wrong question. The claim E5b
   457	carries is structural, not competitive: *hoisted entry checks vs run-to-fault
   458	instrumentation are different latency models, and that difference is exactly why a
   459	dynamic oracle is a post-hoc debugger rather than a runtime guarantee.* Its
   460	per-run slowdown (~1× compute-bound to ~100× memory-heavy, scaling with
   461	instrumented memory traffic, always nonzero) is folded into the E5b latency
   462	measurement rather than quoted as a separate multiplier.
   463	
   464	**Phase-0 gate.** The `coordinator` must confirm the `--runtime-checks off` flag
   465	exists and pin it (step 3). If it does not, E5a is unmeasurable and RQ3's runtime
   466	figure is re-reported as-is with no A/B.
   467	
   468	---

   493	
   494	## 8. 48-hour execution plan (agent-oriented worker cards)
   495	
   496	Each worker below is a **self-contained job card**: when told "you are the
   497	`triton` worker," the card is the complete specification — inputs, tasks,
   498	deliverables, definition-of-done — with no further explanation required.
   499	
   500	**Mandatory onboarding rule (every worker, every time).** Before implementing
   501	anything, a worker must (a) read this whole plan, (b) inspect feasibility of its
   502	own card — the hardest/most-uncertain step first, and (c) list the risks it sees
   503	*for its own lane* in priority order. If anything is unclear, ambiguous, or
   504	blocked, it **asks questions first** — it never silently improvises, guesses a
   505	mapping, or substitutes an operator. Feasibility findings and questions go back
   506	to the `coordinator` (or the owner for §7/§11.7 decision points) before the
   507	first artifact is produced. A worker that skips this gate risks producing
   508	wrong-shaped work; a worker that asks nothing is assumed to have found no
   509	blockers, not to have understood everything.
   510	
   511	### 8.0 Phase 0 — the `coordinator` worker (0–6 h, blocking)
   512	The `coordinator` produces the shared inputs every other worker reads. When told
   513	"you are the `coordinator`," its card is:
   514	
   515	0. **Feasibility inspection:** read the full plan; the highest-risk item is §7
   516	   (two data-integrity discrepancies); if the raw CSV cannot be confirmed, ask
   517	   the owner before proceeding.
   518	1. Reconcile the 2 §7 discrepancies against the raw CSV.
   519	2. Create `benchmark2/settings/` + `benchmark2/specs/` by the §2.5 derivation
   520	   from the original `benchmark/`; record provenance in `manifest.md`.
   521	3. Pin compiler binary + `--dump-ledger`/`--stats` flags + all toolchain versions
   522	   into `manifest.md`; **verify the `--runtime-checks off` flag exists** (E5a
   523	   prerequisite, §6.2) and pin it.
   524	4. Pin the §3.3 mutation-translation table (incl. the TileLang row), the
   525	   "small-input" convention, and the §12 run-harness contract + data/statistics
   526	   schema (§12.3–12.4).
   527	5. Record the machine's **GPU topology** (count, VRAM, MPS on/off) in
   528	   `manifest.md` and assign a `--device` plan per worker (§12.6), so correctness
   529	   lanes share and timing lanes take the exclusivity lock.
   530	6. Confirm the mutation set is fixed to M1/M2/M3 (iteration-validity folded
   531	   into M1; hw-constraint reinstated per owner — see R3) and report before
   532	   E1 dispatch.
   533	
   534	*Deliverables:* `benchmark2/settings/`, `benchmark2/specs/`, `manifest.md`,
   535	the §12.4 statistics manifest + JSON schema, and a **reference `run.sh`
   536	skeleton** (including the `setup` subcommand) workers copy and fill in. The
   537	`coordinator` also records the **shared environment** — CUDA toolkit/nvcc/driver
   538	version + GPU topology — in `manifest.md` as a machine precondition, so no worker
   539	tries to re-install it. *Done when:* a fresh worker can read `settings/` +
   540	`specs/` + the harness contract and start without asking the owner anything.
   541	
   542	### 8.1 Phase 1 — SOTA worker cards (6–36 h, parallel)
   543	
   544	**Work cycle (priority order — applies to every worker, no exceptions).** This
   545	is the explicit loop each agent follows. It never starts a later stage while an
   546	earlier one is incomplete, and it never substitutes an arbitrary operator for
   547	the next priority row.
   548	
   549	| Stage | When | What |
   550	|---|---|---|
   551	| **Level-1 E1** | first, decisive | compose+gate the §3.1 minimal set; mutate the assigned classes; classify every mutant; emit valid JSON |
   552	| **Other work (E2/E3)** | only after level-1 is green | compose+gate all 15 categories; record E2 expressibility + E3 remainder |
   553	| **Level-2 E1** | only after both above | pull the next §3.1 priority row; widen the mutation set |
   554	
   555	**Shared template (every SOTA worker).** Identical shape; only the
   556	per-toolchain row differs. Steps are ordered by the work cycle:
   557	
   558	0. **Feasibility inspection (mandatory, before any work).** Read the full plan;
   559	   inspect this card's hardest step first; list own-lane risks in priority
   560	   order; **ask questions if anything is unclear** (per the §8 onboarding rule).
   561	1. **Compose + gate** the §3.1 minimal coverage set (small-input, §2.3).
   562	2. **Mutate** the classes §3.3 assigns to this toolchain.
   563	3. **Classify** every mutant's outcome ∈ {compile, runtime, never, n/a}.
   564	4. **Compose + gate** the remaining 15 categories (E2/E3 breadth).
   565	5. **Record** E2 expressibility (which classes this surface can even express) and
   566	   E3 remainder (unconditional guards, if any) per the pinned criterion.
   567	6. **Level-2 E1 if idle:** pull the next §3.1 priority row (never before 1–5
   568	   are complete), then classify the new mutants.
   569	7. **Build the harness:** implement `benchmark2/<toolchain>/run.sh` per §12.1
   570	   (subcommands `setup`/`minimal`/`e2`/`e3`/`collect`/`stats`), keeping raw
   571	   artifacts in `raw/`.
   572	8. **Emit + aggregate:** `run.sh collect` → `results/<toolchain>/*.json`, then
   573	   `run.sh stats` → `results/<toolchain>/stats.json` covering its lane's
   574	   statistics (S1 + S8/S9/S12 for SOTA); validate against the §12.3 schema and
   575	   confirm every statistic is traceable to raw JSON.
   576	
   577	**Environment setup (each worker scripts its own toolchain).** The `setup`
   578	subcommand clones the toolchain repo at the commit pinned in `manifest.md` and
   579	builds it, so the lane is reproducible from scratch and a reviewer can re-derive
   580	the exact binary behind every number. **The CUDA build environment is a shared
   581	precondition, not reproduced per worker:** the toolkit/nvcc/driver and the GPU
   582	machine are assumed pre-installed and are recorded (never built) in
   583	`manifest.md`. A worker never reinstalls system CUDA; it only clones + builds its
   584	own toolchain on top of the shared base.
   585	
   586	**Per-toolchain row** (what "you are the X worker" means concretely):
   587	
   588	| Worker | Surface | Mutation classes it runs (§3.3) | E2 expressibility to record | E3 remainder criterion |
   589	|---|---|---|---|---|
   590	| `triton` | Triton → TTIR → PTX | M1 only (M2 = n/a) | masks only; no generated checks | n/a (no generated checks) |
   591	| `mlir-linalg` | linalg named ops + shape inference | M2 only | shape-consistency checks | dynamic shape asserts left unfolded |
   592	| `mlir-low` | scf/affine/memref/vector/gpu | M1 only | bounds guards, transfer masks | unconditional bounds guards after canonicalize |
   593	| `iree` | stream/hal runtime | M1, M2 (entry) | entry shape asserts only | entry checks not statically foldable |
   594	| `tilelang` *(exploratory)* | TileLang DSL | per `coordinator` mapping | shape-aware surface | per pinned criterion |
   595	
   596	**`choreo` worker** (reference lane; runs E1+E2+E3 on our side, same work cycle).
   597	This is the **heaviest single lane**: it carries the reference column for both
   598	mutation classes, the ledger enumeration (all four obligation classes), the
   599	counterfactual, and the entire cost harness — more script work than any SOTA
   600	worker. Budget for it accordingly, and never load it with optional work before
   601	the required lane is green.
   602	1. **Level-1 E1:** implement the 3 mutation families (M1/M2/M3) with equal counts
   603	   on the §3.1 minimal set → choreo column (`c + r = N, never = 0`, §3.1).
   604	2. **Other work:** `--dump-ledger` per operator → E2 generation counts (all 15
   605	   categories); re-verify the no-interval counterfactual (E3 attribution).
   606	3. **Level-2 E1 if idle:** pull the next §3.1 priority row.
   607	4. **Harness:** ship `run.sh` with `setup` (clone choreo @ pinned commit + build)
   608	   and `e4` (compile cost) + `e5` (runtime cost & latency) in addition to the
   609	   §12.1 subcommands, and emit S1–S7, S10 (E4), S13 (E5). **`e5` is required**
   610	   (§6): invoke it after E1–E4 are green, on the exclusivity-locked device
   611	   (§12.6).
   612	
   613	*Deliverable for every Phase-1 worker:* its `benchmark2/<toolchain>/run.sh`,
   614	`results/<toolchain>/` raw JSON + `stats.json`, and composed kernels. *Done
   615	when:* `run.sh all` completes, all 15 categories compile+run (small-input), every
   616	assigned mutant is classified and schema-validated, and `stats.json` reproduces
   617	the statistics for its lane (§12.4).
   618	
   619	### 8.2 Phase 2 — the `integrator` worker (36–44 h)
   620	1. Generate the paper's tables from `benchmark2/results/*/stats.json` (S1–S13),
   621	   never hand-edit.
   622	2. Reconcile abstract/intro/RQ2/RQ6 prose with the corrected numbers.
   623	3. Decide TileLang disclosure (§11.7); record the decision + reason in the artifact.
   624	4. De-AI pass; rebuild ≤11 body pages.
   625	*Dependency: all Phase-1 workers' `stats.json`.*
   626	
   627	### 8.3 Phase 3 — the `qc` worker (44–48 h)
   628	Clean-room spot re-run; undefined-cite check; page-count check; `\Description`
   629	on new floats; regenerate tables from `stats.json` to confirm no drift; verify
   630	that every paper number traces to a `stats.json` value (reject any that don't);
   631	artifact script update.
   632	
   633	**Critical path:** `coordinator` → `choreo` + SOTA workers → `integrator`.
   634	`tilelang` is an off-path lane feeding only the `integrator`'s optional column.
   635	
   636	---
   637	
   638	## 9. Risks & contingencies
   639	
   640	- **R1 (time):** 15 categories × 5 SOTA toolchains × full lifecycle is the
   641	  dominant cost. *Contingency:* small-input mode for exploration; parallelize per
   642	  worker (each SOTA worker owns its own toolchain, no cross-waiting); if a
   643	  toolchain can't compose a category in time, record n/a with reason — never
   644	  fabricate.
   645	- **R2 (page budget):** body is at 11/11. New tables must **replace**
   646	  `tab:rq2-bugs` (extended) and **compress** §5/RQ6 prose (~1 page recoverable),
   647	  not add pages.
   648	- **R3 (M3 hw-constraint surface reach):** `mma`/`tma` alignment and atom
   649	  divisibility are an obligation class in §ledger and are now mutated in E1
   650	  (§3.1), but few SOTA surfaces express them as *checks* (Triton's `tl.dot`
   651	  divisibility, MLIR-low `vector` alignment); most cells will be n/a/partial.
   652	  n/a is evidence for C2, not a weakness. No gate required — the class is
   653	  mutated on choreo (and TileLang, exploratory) regardless.
   654	- **R4 (fairness):** surfaces that can't express a class → n/a. This is evidence
   655	  for C2, not a weakness; n/a is never counted as "detected."
   656	- **R5 (remainder-count subjectivity):** E3's "unconditional guard" must be
   657	  pinned to a documented IR criterion (e.g. a `scf.if`/`affine` bound that
   658	  remains symbolic after canonicalization). The `coordinator` owns the rule.
   659	- **R6 (compose independence):** agents sharing settings but not source could
   660	  still diverge in semantics. *Mitigation:* a shared per-category spec (shapes +
   661	  reference output) is the single source of truth; run-test gate catches drift.
   662	- **R7 (GPU co-tenancy):** workers on one machine can OOM each other or, worse,
   663	  silently corrupt the **cost** numbers (E5) by co-running. *Mitigation:*
   664	  §12.6 — correctness lanes share freely; all timing runs take the exclusivity
   665	  lock and are re-run if `exclusive=false`.
   666	
   667	---
   668	
   669	## 10. Acceptance criteria (what "done" means)
   670	
   671	1. `tab:rq2-bugs` covers **3** classes — M1/M2 (4 paper bug categories) + M3
   672	   (hw-constraint) — equal counts per class, 3-way outcomes, n/a explicit,
   673	   every choreo cell non-zero detection before device execution.
   674	2. `tab:gen-capability` + `tab:per-operator` exist; `layer_norm` = 79/74/5/0;
   675	   SOTA cells are "expressible/n/a" (E2) and remainder counts (E3) — no
   676	   fabricated generation counts.
   677	3. MLIR results are reported as **linalg** and **low-level** separately, against
   678	   their respective mutation sets.
   679	4. Abstract/intro "different kinds of bugs" is now *measured*, or the claim is
   680	   scoped to what is measured.
   681	5. E4 (compile-only) and E5 (runtime A/B + latency) are both required C4 numbers;
   682	   `e5` runs after E1–E4 and its E5a/E5b output refines RQ3's runtime figure.
   683	6. Both Phase-0 discrepancies resolved and recorded in `storyline-state.md`.
   684	7. Body ≤ 11 pages, zero undefined refs, all floats have `\Description`.
   685	
   686	---
   687	
   688	## 11. Additional recommendations (owner asked "any other suggestions?")
   689	
   690	1. **Ground-truth oracle per mutant.** "Never detected" is only meaningful if we
   691	   *prove the mutant actually corrupts output*. Add a manifestation check per
   692	   mutant: run the *unmutated* and *mutated* kernel on the reference input and
   693	   confirm the output differs (or the kernel never writes). Otherwise a "never"
   694	   cell could be a no-op mutant, which is a false success.
   695	2. **Traceability matrix as the authoritative artifact.** Pin §3.3's
   696	   mutation↔surface↔expected-outcome table *before* any agent runs, and diff
   697	   measured vs expected. Surprises (e.g. a SOTA target that *does* catch an
   698	   M1/M2 mutant) are themselves findings, not failures.
   699	3. **Common reporting schema.** One JSON per mutant:
   700	   `{toolchain, category, class, mutant_id, outcome ∈ {compile,runtime,never,n/a},
   701	   artifact_hash}`. The full record set and statistics are specified in §12.3–
   702	   §12.4 (the authoritative schema); this line is the E1 subset. All tables are
   703	   *generated* from these files, so the paper and the artifact can never drift.
   704	4. **Keep small/full runs semantically paired.** Record both; the full-size number
   705	   is the cited one, but the small-size run must pass the same reference check so
   706	   "small" is never a different program.
   707	5. **Version pinning is part of the deliverable.** The artifact script must
   708	   reproduce choreo + Triton + MLIR + IREE versions and flags exactly; reviewers
   709	   will re-run this. The per-toolchain `setup` subcommand (§8.1, §12.1) makes
   710	   this reproducible: clone at the pinned commit and build on the shared CUDA
   711	   base.
   712	6. **Confirm "scripts" is not a category.** See §2.1 — if it *is* an operator,
   713	   the suite is 16 categories and every downstream count shifts by one row.
   714	7. **TileLang disclosure must be decided, not defaulted.** Run TileLang in the
   715	   exploratory lane. If it is excluded at integration, the reason (e.g. "surface
   716	   cannot express the obligation classes; results are n/a") must be recorded in
   717	   the artifact, so the omission is a documented scoping decision rather than an
   718	   un-reported baseline. ASPLOS reviewers routinely ask "did you compare against
   719	   TileLang?" — the safest answer is a measured n/a, not silence.
   720	
   721	---
   722	
   723	## 12. Run harness (shell scripts) & data-collection schema
   724	
   725	Every worker ships **one shell harness** per toolchain at
   726	`benchmark2/<toolchain>/run.sh`, with a fixed subcommand surface. Runs and
   727	collection are **separate stages**: `run` produces raw artifacts, `collect`
   728	parses them into the JSON schema, `stats` aggregates JSON into the
   729	paper-facing numbers. This separation is what lets a reviewer re-run and lets
   730	the `integrator` regenerate tables with zero hand-editing.
   731	
   732	### 12.1 Harness contract (identical across all workers)
   733	
   734	```
   735	benchmark2/<toolchain>/run.sh <subcommand>
   736	
   737	  setup      # one-time: clone the toolchain repo @ pinned commit + build
   738	  minimal    # level-1 E1: compose+gate the §3.1 minimal set, mutate, classify
   739	  e2         # breadth: compose+gate all 15 categories; record expressibility
   740	  e3         # record remainder (unconditional guards) per pinned criterion
   741	  e4         # choreo only: compile cost
   742	  e5         # choreo only: runtime residue overhead (E5a) + detection latency (E5b)
   743	  collect    # parse raw logs/IR/dumps → results/<toolchain>/*.json (schema §12.3)
   744	  stats      # aggregate JSON → results/<toolchain>/stats.json (+ per-table CSV)
   745	  all        # minimal → e2 → e3 → collect → stats (choreo appends e4 → e5)
   746	```
   747	
   748	Rules every harness must obey:
   749	- `minimal` must succeed (all cells classified, JSON valid) before `e2`/`e3`.
   750	- Level-2 E1 is invoked as `run.sh minimal --level2` and only after `e2`+`e3`
   751	  are green (§8.1 work cycle).
   752	- `e5` is **required** and is in `all`: it is long-running, so the `choreo`
   753	  worker schedules it after E1–E4 are green and runs it on the
   754	  exclusivity-locked device (§12.6). E4's compile number and E5's runtime A/B
   755	  together are the C4 evidence (§6).
   756	- `--small` is the default for exploration; `--full` is required for the final
   757	  recorded numbers and must re-run the same reference check (§2.4).
   758	- Raw artifacts (compile logs, IR dumps, exit codes, output diffs) are kept
   759	  under `benchmark2/<toolchain>/raw/` and are never deleted — `collect` is
   760	  idempotent and re-parses them.
   761	- **Device pinning:** every run takes `--device <i>` (default from
   762	  `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`); the value is recorded in every raw record (§12.6).
   763	- `setup` is idempotent and one-time: it clones the toolchain repo at the commit
   764	  pinned in `manifest.md` and builds it. It never installs the CUDA build
   765	  environment (toolkit/nvcc/driver) — that is a shared, pre-installed machine
   766	  precondition recorded in `manifest.md` (§8.1).
   767	
   768	### 12.6 GPU co-tenancy (workers sharing one machine)
   769	
   770	Workers may share one GPU machine. The GPU's own mechanism does **not** make
   771	that safe for everything — it isolates the *wrong* axis for our purposes:
   772	
   773	| What the GPU does | Consequence for us |
   774	|---|---|
   775	| Time-slices / MPS-multiplexes concurrent processes | discrete outcomes are fine; **timing is garbage** under contention |
   776	| Shared VRAM, no per-process cap | two workers allocating big tensors can **OOM each other** |
   777	| No clock/thermals isolation | sustained co-load changes boost/clock → non-reproducible cost numbers |
   778	| `compute-sanitizer` serializes kernels | running it co-resident perturbs everyone |
   779	
   780	**Correctness outcomes are contention-insensitive; cost is not.** E1/E2/E3
   781	produce *discrete* results (compiles/refutes/fires, generation counts, remainder
   782	counts) — a check fires or it doesn't, independent of who else is on the GPU. So
   783	those lanes may share freely. **E5 (runtime residue cost + detection latency) is
   784	GPU timing** — it must run under **exclusive** conditions or the number is
   785	meaningless. (E4 compile cost is host-CPU time, not GPU-contended, but still
   786	measured under quiet host load.)
   787	
   788	**Rules:**
   789	1. Pin each worker to a distinct `--device` where multi-GPU exists; on a single
   790	   GPU, correctness lanes may overlap but must not exceed VRAM (use `--small`).
   791	2. `e5` (and any timing) runs only under an **exclusivity lock**
   792	   (`benchmark2/.gpu-lock`): no other `run.sh` may be active, `nvidia-smi` shows
   793	   the device otherwise idle, and the run records `exclusive=true`. The `qc`
   794	   worker rejects any `cost` record with `exclusive=false`.
   795	3. Record `gpu_device`, `exclusive∈{true,false}`, and `nvidia-smi` snapshot
   796	   (util/mem/clock) in the `kernel`/`cost` records so a reviewer can see the run
   797	   conditions.
   798	4. The `coordinator` records the machine's GPU topology (count, VRAM, MPS
   799	   on/off) in `manifest.md`; workers read it before picking `--device`.
   800	
   801	The paper's numbers are the *target outputs*, not afterthoughts. If we don't pin
   802	the exact statistics now, each worker will record a slightly different thing and
   803	the `integrator` cannot merge lanes. The manifest below is the contract: a
   804	worker's `stats.json` must reproduce the listed aggregates, and the `integrator`
   805	writes no number into `main.tex` that is not derivable from some worker's
   806	`stats.json`.
   807	
   808	### 12.3 Raw record schema (one JSON line per observation)
   809	
   810	| Record | Fields | Produced by |
   811	|---|---|---|
   812	| `kernel` | `toolchain, category, settings_hash, kernel_hash, size∈{small,full}, gpu_device, exclusive∈{true,false}, compile∈{ok,fail,detected}, run∈{ok,crash}, ref_check∈{pass,fail}` | `minimal`, `e2` |
   813	| `mutant` | `toolchain, category, class∈{M1,M2}, paper_category∈{dim-mismatch,oob,wrong-shape,stride}, mutant_id, level∈{1,2}, outcome∈{compile,runtime,never,n/a}, stage∈{compile,runtime} (choreo only), manifest∈{corrupts,noop}` | `minimal` (+`--level2`) |
   814	| `sanitizer` | `toolchain∈{triton,mlir-linalg,mlir-low,iree,tilelang}, category, class, mutant_id, flagged∈{true,false}, fault∈{oob,uninit,misaligned,none}, exercised∈{true,false}` | `minimal` (SOTA targets only, `--tool memcheck`) |
   815	| `obligation` | `toolchain=choreo, category, kernel_id, obligation_id, class∈{elem,shape,loop,hw}, outcome∈{proven,refuted,runtime,budgeted}, mechanism∈{canonical,interval,direct}` | `e2`/`e3` (choreo) |
   816	| `expressibility` | `toolchain, category, class, expressible∈{yes,no,partial}` | `e2` (SOTA) |
   817	| `remainder` | `toolchain, category, unconditional_guards (count), criterion_ref` | `e3` (SOTA) |
   818	| `cost` | `toolchain=choreo, category, compile_overhead_pct, bucket` | `e4` |
   819	| `residue` | `toolchain=choreo, category, checks∈{on,off}, runtime_ms, gpu_device, exclusive∈{true,false}` | `e5` (E5a) |
   820	| `latency` | `detector∈{choreo-entry,compute-sanitizer}, category, mutant_id, check_loc∈{entry,interior}, time_to_report_us, gpu_device, exclusive∈{true,false}` | `e5` (E5b) |
   821	
   822	### 12.4 Statistics manifest (what `stats` must emit)
   823	
   824	Each statistic is tagged with the paper table/number it feeds, so the
   825	`integrator`'s mapping is mechanical.
   826	
   827	| # | Statistic | Aggregation | Feeds |
   828	|---|---|---|---|
   829	| S1 | detection matrix | per class × toolchain: `n_injected, n_compile, n_runtime, n_never, n_na` | `tab:rq2-bugs` |
   830	| S12 | sanitizer supplement | per class × SOTA toolchain: `flagged ∧ exercised` mutants (delta vs bare SOTA) | `tab:rq2-bugs` sanitizer column (§3.5) |
   831	| S2 | choreo before-device | `Σ(n_compile + n_runtime)` per class; **never = 0** asserted | abstract "210/210" |
   832	| S3 | generation totals | choreo obligations per class, per category, + grand total | `tab:gen-capability`, abstract "17,717" |
   833	| S4 | per-operator ledger | per category: `expressed / discharged / runtime / budgeted / dropped(=0)` | `tab:per-operator`; `layer_norm`=79/74/5/0 |
   834	| S5 | discharge rate | `Σ discharged / Σ generated` overall, **and split by static-shape vs dynamic-shape** | RQ1; "93.2%", "99.2%/87.9%" |
   835	| S6 | mechanism split | per outcome: `canonical / interval / direct` counts | `tab:rq6-mechanism` |
   836	| S7 | no-interval counterfactual | re-run with interval reasoning disabled; report Δ rate | "93.2%→77.2%" |
   837	| S8 | SOTA expressibility | per class × toolchain: `expressible` counts | `tab:gen-capability` |
   838	| S9 | SOTA remainder | per toolchain: `Σ unconditional_guards` per category | `tab:per-operator` (SOTA cols) |
   839	| S10 | compile cost | median compile-overhead % (choreo) | RQ4 |
   840	| S13 | runtime cost & latency | E5a: Δ(residue on/off) per dynamic-shape case; E5b: choreo-entry vs sanitizer time-to-report | RQ3 + E5 |
   841	| S11 | integrity register | the §7 4-way split (compile/runtime/timeout/never) re-derived from raw CSV | Phase-0 gate |
   842	
   843	**Non-negotiable cross-cutting fields** (every record, every worker): the
   844	`settings_hash` + `kernel_hash` + toolchain version from `manifest.md`. Without
   845	these, a number cannot be traced to a build and is treated as unverified by the
   846	`qc` worker.
   847	
   848	### 12.5 How the agent works "right" because of this
   849	
   850	A worker is judged correct iff, after `run.sh all`: (a) `minimal` cells are
   851	complete and schema-valid, (b) `stats.json` emits exactly the statistics its
   852	lane owns (§12.4), (c) every `stats.json` number is traceable to raw JSON via a
   853	`stats` log of the aggregation. If a worker's harness cannot produce a required
   854	statistic, that is a **feasibility finding to report** (per the §8 onboarding
   855	rule), not a silent omission.
   856	
   857	---
   858	
   859	## Appendix A — Commands the executing agents need (to confirm/extend)
   860	
   861	> The primary entry point is the per-toolchain harness `benchmark2/<toolchain>/run.sh`
   862	> (§12.1); the raw commands below are what the harness wraps. The `coordinator`
   863	> confirms/pins these and records exact versions/flags in `manifest.md`.
   864	
   865	```bash
   866	# per-toolchain environment setup (clone @ pinned commit + build; NOT system CUDA)
   867	benchmark2/<toolchain>/run.sh setup
   868	
   869	# choreo ledger per kernel (class, outcome, cost)
   870	choreo --dump-ledger path/to/kernel.co
   871	
   872	# suite-wide stats (gen/discharged/residue + cost buckets)
   873	python scripts/choreo_assertion_stats.py --suite benchmark/choreo --output results/x.csv
   874	
   875	# MLIR linalg-level (shape) + low-level (affine/memref/vector) probes
   876	mlir-opt --pass-pipeline='builtin.module(func.func(...))' kernel.mlir
   877	iree-compile --iree-hal-target-backends=cuda kernel.mlir -o kernel.vmfb
   878	iree-run-module --module=kernel.vmfb --function=main --device=cuda
   879	```
   880	*The `coordinator` must pin exact versions/flags and record them in the artifact script.*
   881

# ASPLOS 2027 Action Plan

> **⚠️ SUPERSEDED (2026-08-25):** This plan's VN-centric framing was abandoned after the
> ablation showed value numbering contributes 0.0 pp to discharge. The authoritative plan
> is now **`rewrite-blueprint.md`** (full rewrite: "The Compiler as Auditor" /
> safety-ledger framing); findings in **`ablation-and-framing-notes.md`**. The experiment
> infrastructure and RQ1–RQ3/RQ5 results described below remain valid and carry over.

**Deadline:** September 9, 2026 (~3 weeks)  
**Main claim:** Comprehensive compile-time safety checking for GPU kernels is practical when constraint generation and resolution co-locate at the semantic stage, with residual obligations managed through a tunable assessment abstraction.  
**Title constraint:** Do not use "Croqtile" in the title.  
**Primary focus:** Verification/assessment architecture, not the surface language.

---

## 0. Pre-Flight Checklist (Before Week 1)

- [ ] Confirm final title from `title-and-scope.md` options.
